### [00:06:49.220 - 00:07:02.180]

### [00:07:03.380 - 00:07:07.440]

### [00:07:26.420 - 00:07:41.780]

### [00:07:41.780 - 00:07:50.260]

### [00:07:50.260 - 00:08:00.120]

### [00:08:00.120 - 00:08:13.320]

### [00:08:13.320 - 00:08:23.780]

But in modern-day RL-HF training, this K-L divergence is typically applied to the base model, which is the SFT model.

### [00:08:34.940 - 00:08:47.600]

### [00:45:25.910 - 00:45:27.250]

And you're exactly right.

### [00:45:27.250 - 00:45:32.390]

When you generate these solutions multiple times,

### [00:45:33.090 - 00:45:39.710]

something that will influence your results is how diverse the solutions that you will generate will be.

### [00:45:40.610 - 00:45:44.550]

And this is something that you indeed use the temperature for.

### [00:45:46.250 - 00:45:51.770]

Now, okay, so what is the relationship between temperature and pass at k?

### [00:45:52.770 - 00:45:54.870]

Do we want a low temperature?

### [00:45:54.870 - 00:45:56.490]

Do we want a high temperature?

### [00:45:57.250 - 00:45:58.890]

Or do we want something in between?

### [00:46:00.550 - 00:46:03.610]

Well, if you take a very low temperature,

### [00:46:04.970 - 00:46:10.290]

you know that your solutions will be good, but they will not be diverse.

### [00:46:11.430 - 00:46:15.550]

So if you increase the number of samples that you generate,

### [00:46:16.090 - 00:46:17.690]

that quantity will not change.

### [00:46:17.690 - 00:46:22.850]

which is seen with this graph with the T equal to zero, it doesn't change.

### [00:46:23.570 - 00:46:28.890]

But then when you have T equal to 0.2, now it increases because you have some diversity.

### [00:46:30.980 - 00:46:33.220]

But if you increase this temperature too much,

### [00:46:34.220 - 00:46:36.780]

then you will have the diversity that you want.

### [00:46:36.780 - 00:46:45.700]

But they will also harm the performance of your predictions because maybe the tokens that were not that likely would become likely.

### [00:46:46.960 - 00:46:51.820]

And so here on the opposite side, we have, I don't know, T equal 1.2 here,

### [00:46:51.880 - 00:46:55.440]

which is extreme in this example, which is not the best.

### [00:46:55.440 - 00:47:02.340]

So I guess to respond to you, a typical temperature choice would be something that is not too large, not too small.

### [00:47:03.080 - 00:47:06.420]

And I believe in this example is T equal to 0.8,

### [00:47:06.840 - 00:47:11.700]

that I think produces the best results towards the bigger samples.

### [00:47:11.700 - 00:47:14.020]

But then here, I guess it's not clear, maybe 0.4.

### [00:47:16.240 - 00:47:18.700]

So that's why you will see that in papers,

### [00:47:19.320 - 00:47:27.240]

people always specify the temperature that they choose to produce the benchmark results.

### [00:47:28.460 - 00:47:31.240]

So that's why it's good to have that in mind.

### [00:47:33.480 - 00:47:33.820]

Cool.

### [00:47:33.980 - 00:47:37.940]

So these are the main metrics that people use, but not the only ones.

### [00:47:38.100 - 00:47:39.800]

There is another one that you may see,

### [00:47:40.400 - 00:47:42.800]

which is called consensus at K.

### [00:47:44.580 - 00:47:52.160]

which is the answer that is coming from the highest number of times

### [00:47:52.160 - 00:47:54.700]

that the answer appears among your generations.

### [00:47:56.400 - 00:48:00.400]

And you can think of the self-consistency technique as being very related to that.

### [00:48:01.900 - 00:48:03.300]

So, yeah, so you may see that.

are typically the ones that you're familiar with,

like accuracy, exact match, and so on.

And I guess just like that,

### [00:57:53.060 - 00:57:55.520]

I guess we saw that the way

### [00:57:55.520 - 00:57:59.080]

that we want to scale our model

### [00:57:59.080 - 00:58:00.240]

is with RL.

### [00:58:01.800 - 00:58:03.580]

I guess is everyone convinced of that?

### [00:58:03.720 - 00:58:04.700]

Did I convince you well

### [00:58:04.700 - 00:58:05.900]

with those arguments?

### [00:58:06.120 - 00:58:08.280]

Or does anyone have any questions on this?

### [00:58:08.280 - 00:58:10.360]

And I guess here,

### [00:58:10.540 - 00:58:13.060]

I want you to have a takeaway,

### [00:58:13.240 - 00:58:16.460]

which is we want to use RL

### [00:58:16.460 - 00:58:20.340]

to incentivize our model to think more

### [00:58:20.340 - 00:58:23.460]

if we start from scratch,

### [00:58:23.620 - 00:58:25.160]

meaning we don't have reasoning chains.

### [00:58:26.780 - 00:58:28.460]

So is everyone good with this?

### [00:58:31.140 - 00:58:31.280]

Yeah?

### [00:58:33.800 - 00:58:34.680]

Yeah, kind of yeah?

### [00:58:34.820 - 00:58:34.940]

Okay.

### [00:58:35.420 - 00:58:36.640]

So I'll take this as a yes.

### [00:58:37.800 - 00:58:39.220]

So now we're going to see

### [00:58:39.220 - 00:58:41.340]

the algorithm that we use

### [00:58:41.340 - 00:58:44.240]

to perform that RL step.

### [00:58:45.340 - 00:58:48.720]

And you may have heard this acronym a lot.

### [00:58:49.560 - 00:58:51.820]

So this algorithm that we will see

### [00:58:51.820 - 00:58:53.020]

is called GRPO.

### [00:58:54.520 - 00:58:57.120]

And GRPO has been released in 2024.

### [00:58:58.400 - 00:59:02.380]

And it has been kind of the go-to RL algorithm

### [00:59:02.380 - 00:59:04.760]

for all these reasoning tasks

### [00:59:04.760 - 00:59:07.680]

or for these reasoning training tasks.

### [00:59:08.680 - 00:59:10.060]

So what is GRPO?

### [00:59:11.340 - 00:59:13.180]

So GRPO stands for

### [00:59:13.180 - 00:59:17.020]

Group Relative Policy Optimization.

### [00:59:18.300 - 00:59:21.640]

And it is an RL algorithm

### [00:59:21.640 - 00:59:24.320]

which very much like PPO

### [00:59:24.320 - 00:59:27.760]

aims to do these two things we talked about.

### [00:59:28.720 - 00:59:30.560]

So the first one is

### [00:59:30.560 - 00:59:33.560]

to maximize advantages.

### [00:59:33.560 - 00:59:39.220]

Advantage is something that tells you

### [00:59:39.220 - 00:59:41.840]

whether what you're producing as an answer

### [00:59:41.840 - 00:59:45.360]

is better than what you would expect.

### [00:59:47.040 - 00:59:49.740]

So this is the first part.

### [00:59:49.820 - 00:59:51.200]

And then the second part is

### [00:59:51.200 - 00:59:52.600]

that you do not want

### [00:59:52.600 - 00:59:55.240]

to deviate too much

### [00:59:55.240 - 00:59:59.500]

from either the old model

### [00:59:59.500 - 01:00:00.620]

which is the model

### [01:00:00.620 - 01:00:02.480]

at the previous RL iteration

### [01:00:02.480 - 01:00:05.200]

or the base model

### [01:00:05.200 - 01:00:08.400]

which is the model at SFT stage.

### [01:00:10.080 - 01:00:12.080]

So GRPO is also

### [01:00:12.080 - 01:00:14.360]

doing the same thing as PPO

### [01:00:14.360 - 01:00:15.040]

in that sense.

### [01:00:15.040 - 01:00:18.100]

But then there is a key difference.

### [01:00:19.320 - 01:00:20.600]

And that difference lies

### [01:00:20.600 - 01:00:22.620]

in how it computes the advantage.

### [01:00:24.160 - 01:00:25.080]

So if you remember

### [01:00:25.080 - 01:00:26.660]

PPO

### [01:00:26.660 - 01:00:29.380]

estimated the advantage

### [01:00:29.380 - 01:00:31.680]

by taking the reward

### [01:00:31.680 - 01:00:33.700]

which is at the completion level.

### [01:00:34.420 - 01:00:36.040]

So you know you have your prompts

### [01:00:36.040 - 01:00:38.240]

and you have a response

### [01:00:38.240 - 01:00:39.300]

a full response

### [01:00:39.300 - 01:00:41.420]

and then based on

### [01:00:41.420 - 01:00:42.460]

those two elements

### [01:00:42.460 - 01:00:44.020]

you have a reward.

### [01:00:45.360 - 01:00:46.560]

So what PPO did

### [01:00:46.560 - 01:00:49.280]

was take that into consideration

### [01:00:49.280 - 01:00:51.800]

along with something else

### [01:00:51.800 - 01:00:53.920]

that it was training jointly

### [01:00:53.920 - 01:00:55.660]

at training time

### [01:00:55.660 - 01:00:56.200]

which was called

### [01:00:56.200 - 01:00:57.280]

the value function.

### [01:00:58.960 - 01:01:01.020]

And the value function objective

### [01:01:01.020 - 01:01:03.980]

was to try to predict

### [01:01:03.980 - 01:01:06.240]

what that reward was

### [01:01:06.240 - 01:01:08.640]

if we were to continue

### [01:01:08.640 - 01:01:10.760]

generating the tokens

### [01:01:10.760 - 01:01:12.400]

following the policy.

### [01:01:14.260 - 01:01:14.940]

And if you remember

### [01:01:14.940 - 01:01:16.440]

the advantage here

### [01:01:16.440 - 01:01:17.180]

is actually

### [01:01:17.180 - 01:01:18.480]

in the case of PPO

### [01:01:18.480 - 01:01:20.260]

was something that

### [01:01:20.260 - 01:01:21.060]

we obtained

### [01:01:21.060 - 01:01:22.520]

based on

### [01:01:22.520 - 01:01:23.840]

a very complex formula

### [01:01:23.840 - 01:01:25.060]

that we did not see actually

### [01:01:25.060 - 01:01:25.980]

but that's called

### [01:01:25.980 - 01:01:27.460]

the generalized

### [01:01:27.460 - 01:01:29.140]

advantage estimation

### [01:01:29.140 - 01:01:30.180]

that is a method

### [01:01:30.180 - 01:01:33.000]

to estimate

### [01:01:33.000 - 01:01:33.740]

those advantages

### [01:01:33.740 - 01:01:36.420]

and the big limitation

### [01:01:36.420 - 01:01:37.000]

with that

### [01:01:37.000 - 01:01:38.260]

was that you had

### [01:01:38.260 - 01:01:39.320]

to train

### [01:01:39.320 - 01:01:40.320]

a value function

### [01:01:40.320 - 01:01:42.060]

jointly with your policy.

### [01:01:43.160 - 01:01:43.580]

So this was

### [01:01:43.580 - 01:01:44.600]

this big bottleneck

### [01:01:44.600 - 01:01:46.700]

and in contrast

### [01:01:46.700 - 01:01:47.280]

to that

### [01:01:47.280 - 01:01:49.440]

GRPO says

### [01:01:49.440 - 01:01:50.480]

well

### [01:01:50.480 - 01:01:51.440]

we're not going

### [01:01:51.440 - 01:01:51.940]

to do that.

### [01:01:53.040 - 01:01:54.360]

We're going to take

### [01:01:54.360 - 01:01:55.080]

a completion

### [01:01:55.080 - 01:01:57.260]

we're going to compute

### [01:01:57.260 - 01:01:58.100]

each reward

### [01:01:58.100 - 01:02:00.140]

and we're going

### [01:02:00.140 - 01:02:01.020]

to compare it

### [01:02:01.020 - 01:02:02.680]

with the average

### [01:02:02.680 - 01:02:03.640]

percentage of rewards

### [01:02:03.640 - 01:02:05.680]

of completions

### [01:02:05.680 - 01:02:06.940]

for that same prompt.

### [01:02:07.480 - 01:02:09.800]

So I'll give you

### [01:02:09.800 - 01:02:10.180]

an example.

### [01:02:10.800 - 01:02:11.800]

So let's suppose

### [01:02:11.800 - 01:02:13.420]

we have a math problem.

### [01:02:14.640 - 01:02:15.940]

So what we're saying

### [01:02:15.940 - 01:02:16.300]

here

### [01:02:16.300 - 01:02:17.740]

is

### [01:02:17.740 - 01:02:19.520]

we're going to generate

### [01:02:19.520 - 01:02:21.540]

multiple completions

### [01:02:21.540 - 01:02:22.800]

for that same prompt

### [01:02:22.800 - 01:02:26.410]

and for each prompt

### [01:02:26.410 - 01:02:28.310]

and completion

### [01:02:28.310 - 01:02:29.810]

and the prompt

### [01:02:29.810 - 01:02:30.330]

is shared.

### [01:02:30.330 - 01:02:31.890]

so for each completion

### [01:02:31.890 - 01:02:33.650]

we're going

### [01:02:33.650 - 01:02:34.850]

to measure

### [01:02:34.850 - 01:02:35.910]

I guess

### [01:02:35.910 - 01:02:36.570]

a relative

### [01:02:36.570 - 01:02:38.910]

measure

### [01:02:38.910 - 01:02:39.530]

of

### [01:02:39.530 - 01:02:40.490]

the reward

### [01:02:40.490 - 01:02:41.350]

for that completion

### [01:02:41.350 - 01:02:43.230]

and all the other

### [01:02:43.230 - 01:02:43.710]

rewards

### [01:02:43.710 - 01:02:44.570]

of the other

### [01:02:44.570 - 01:02:45.110]

completions.

### [01:02:47.260 - 01:02:48.420]

So this will give us

### [01:02:48.420 - 01:02:49.000]

an idea

### [01:02:49.000 - 01:02:50.400]

of how much

### [01:02:50.400 - 01:02:50.740]

better

### [01:02:50.740 - 01:02:51.680]

or how much

### [01:02:51.680 - 01:02:52.120]

worse

### [01:02:52.120 - 01:02:53.560]

is it from

### [01:02:53.560 - 01:02:54.560]

I guess

### [01:02:54.560 - 01:02:55.440]

the rest of the group

### [01:02:55.440 - 01:02:57.340]

and so this

### [01:02:57.340 - 01:02:58.160]

is going to be

### [01:02:58.160 - 01:02:59.080]

our advantage.

### [01:02:59.080 - 01:03:03.300]

So the big

### [01:03:03.300 - 01:03:04.320]

benefit here

### [01:03:04.320 - 01:03:06.140]

is that we do

### [01:03:06.140 - 01:03:07.000]

not use value

### [01:03:07.000 - 01:03:07.600]

functions

### [01:03:07.600 - 01:03:09.080]

we do not use

### [01:03:09.080 - 01:03:09.240]

that

### [01:03:09.240 - 01:03:09.900]

the only thing

### [01:03:09.900 - 01:03:10.300]

we do

### [01:03:10.300 - 01:03:10.920]

is

### [01:03:10.920 - 01:03:13.100]

we sample

### [01:03:13.100 - 01:03:14.540]

not just one

### [01:03:14.540 - 01:03:15.620]

but multiple

### [01:03:15.620 - 01:03:16.380]

completions

### [01:03:16.380 - 01:03:17.860]

in order to

### [01:03:17.860 - 01:03:18.640]

compute the

### [01:03:18.640 - 01:03:19.160]

average

### [01:03:19.160 - 01:03:20.580]

of the rewards

### [01:03:20.580 - 01:03:21.120]

of the group

### [01:03:21.120 - 01:03:26.330]

does this make

### [01:03:26.330 - 01:03:26.610]

sense?

### [01:03:30.030 - 01:03:30.370]

Oh yeah

### [01:03:30.370 - 01:03:39.420]

so what would

### [01:03:39.420 - 01:03:39.960]

you do instead?

### [01:03:46.180 - 01:03:46.780]

So the question

### [01:03:46.780 - 01:03:47.460]

is why would

### [01:03:47.460 - 01:03:47.940]

you do that?

### [01:03:48.720 - 01:03:49.780]

Well the traditional

### [01:03:49.780 - 01:03:50.920]

RL algorithms

### [01:03:50.920 - 01:03:52.520]

just for reference

### [01:03:52.520 - 01:03:53.680]

they have been

### [01:03:53.680 - 01:03:54.200]

developed

### [01:03:54.200 - 01:03:55.240]

before all

### [01:03:55.240 - 01:03:56.220]

these LLMs

### [01:03:56.220 - 01:03:57.480]

became a thing

### [01:03:57.480 - 01:03:58.800]

so for instance

### [01:03:58.800 - 01:03:59.820]

PPU was

### [01:03:59.820 - 01:04:00.480]

a method

### [01:04:00.480 - 01:04:01.060]

that I believe

### [01:04:01.060 - 01:04:02.100]

is from 2017

### [01:04:02.100 - 01:04:04.580]

so these quantities

### [01:04:04.580 - 01:04:05.600]

they may make

### [01:04:05.600 - 01:04:06.400]

a lot of sense

### [01:04:06.400 - 01:04:07.440]

in some setups

### [01:04:07.440 - 01:04:08.820]

but maybe not

### [01:04:08.820 - 01:04:09.360]

in the language

### [01:04:09.360 - 01:04:09.720]

world

### [01:04:09.720 - 01:04:10.980]

and I guess

### [01:04:10.980 - 01:04:12.280]

here the idea

### [01:04:12.280 - 01:04:13.520]

is

### [01:04:13.520 - 01:04:15.140]

let's try to

### [01:04:15.140 - 01:04:16.020]

think of something

### [01:04:16.020 - 01:04:17.700]

that tells you

### [01:04:17.700 - 01:04:19.000]

how good

### [01:04:19.000 - 01:04:20.340]

your completion

### [01:04:20.340 - 01:04:20.840]

is

### [01:04:20.840 - 01:04:21.620]

just in the

### [01:04:21.620 - 01:04:22.400]

relative sense

### [01:04:22.400 - 01:04:24.160]

but here I guess

### [01:04:24.160 - 01:04:24.820]

what the authors

### [01:04:24.820 - 01:04:25.480]

want to do

### [01:04:25.480 - 01:04:26.780]

is to not have

### [01:04:26.780 - 01:04:27.940]

to have this

### [01:04:27.940 - 01:04:28.820]

very expensive

### [01:04:28.820 - 01:04:30.180]

joint model

### [01:04:30.180 - 01:04:30.720]

that they have

### [01:04:30.720 - 01:04:31.180]

to train

### [01:04:31.180 - 01:04:33.400]

and think of

### [01:04:33.400 - 01:04:34.320]

another relative

### [01:04:34.320 - 01:04:34.960]

way of doing

### [01:04:34.960 - 01:04:35.260]

that

### [01:04:35.260 - 01:04:36.400]

so I think

### [01:04:36.400 - 01:04:36.780]

that's the

### [01:04:36.780 - 01:04:37.240]

high level

### [01:04:37.240 - 01:04:37.720]

intuition

### [01:04:37.720 - 01:04:39.920]

and I guess

### [01:04:39.920 - 01:04:40.700]

here the idea

### [01:04:40.700 - 01:04:41.280]

is

### [01:04:41.280 - 01:04:43.600]

let's just

### [01:04:43.600 - 01:04:44.380]

sample multiple

### [01:04:44.380 - 01:04:44.880]

times

### [01:04:44.880 - 01:04:46.120]

and see

### [01:04:46.120 - 01:04:47.660]

how one

### [01:04:47.660 - 01:04:48.300]

response

### [01:04:48.300 - 01:04:49.420]

is comparing

### [01:04:49.420 - 01:04:50.260]

to the others

### [01:04:50.260 - 01:04:51.780]

and then use

### [01:04:51.780 - 01:04:52.400]

that as some

### [01:04:52.400 - 01:04:53.240]

kind of baseline

### [01:04:53.240 - 01:04:56.220]

to put this

### [01:04:56.220 - 01:04:56.920]

reward into

### [01:04:56.920 - 01:04:57.420]

context

### [01:04:57.420 - 01:04:58.260]

so for instance

### [01:04:58.260 - 01:04:59.720]

you may have

### [01:04:59.720 - 01:05:01.520]

a math problem

### [01:05:01.520 - 01:05:02.120]

that's kind of

### [01:05:02.120 - 01:05:02.480]

easy

### [01:05:02.480 - 01:05:05.140]

and if you

### [01:05:05.140 - 01:05:05.680]

have a high

### [01:05:05.680 - 01:05:06.440]

reward for that

### [01:05:06.440 - 01:05:06.700]

one

### [01:05:06.700 - 01:05:07.960]

maybe it's

### [01:05:07.960 - 01:05:08.400]

because the

### [01:05:08.400 - 01:05:08.820]

problem was

### [01:05:08.820 - 01:05:09.520]

easy to start

### [01:05:09.520 - 01:05:09.760]

with

### [01:05:09.760 - 01:05:11.600]

but if you

### [01:05:11.600 - 01:05:12.300]

have let's

### [01:05:12.300 - 01:05:12.680]

say

### [01:05:12.680 - 01:05:14.500]

very hard

### [01:05:14.500 - 01:05:14.900]

problem

### [01:05:14.900 - 01:05:16.060]

if you have

### [01:05:16.060 - 01:05:16.760]

the correct

### [01:05:16.760 - 01:05:17.220]

answer

### [01:05:17.220 - 01:05:18.060]

then you want

### [01:05:18.060 - 01:05:18.720]

to make sure

### [01:05:18.720 - 01:05:20.540]

you're incentivizing

### [01:05:20.540 - 01:05:21.260]

your model

### [01:05:21.260 - 01:05:22.580]

to upweight

### [01:05:22.580 - 01:05:23.700]

those tokens

### [01:05:23.700 - 01:05:24.260]

much more

### [01:05:24.260 - 01:05:24.640]

because

### [01:05:24.640 - 01:05:26.580]

if you find

### [01:05:26.580 - 01:05:27.260]

a good solution

### [01:05:27.260 - 01:05:27.880]

it's a good

### [01:05:27.880 - 01:05:28.860]

solution to a

### [01:05:28.860 - 01:05:29.580]

hard problem

### [01:05:29.580 - 01:05:30.700]

so you want

### [01:05:30.700 - 01:05:31.360]

to somehow

### [01:05:31.360 - 01:05:32.120]

bake that in

### [01:05:32.120 - 01:05:33.160]

into your

### [01:05:33.160 - 01:05:33.620]

advantage

### [01:05:33.620 - 01:05:34.660]

so that's

### [01:05:34.660 - 01:05:35.000]

kind of the

### [01:05:35.000 - 01:05:35.440]

intuition

### [01:05:35.440 - 01:05:37.860]

so GRPO

### [01:05:37.860 - 01:05:38.540]

was developed

### [01:05:38.540 - 01:05:39.300]

a year ago

### [01:05:39.300 - 01:05:40.540]

and a year

### [01:05:40.540 - 01:05:40.880]

ago

### [01:05:40.880 - 01:05:41.160]

you know

### [01:05:41.160 - 01:05:41.520]

LLMs

### [01:05:41.520 - 01:05:42.180]

were everywhere

### [01:05:42.180 - 01:05:43.580]

so this is

### [01:05:43.580 - 01:05:44.460]

also taking

### [01:05:44.460 - 01:05:45.440]

into consideration

### [01:05:45.440 - 01:05:47.060]

I guess

### [01:05:47.060 - 01:05:47.980]

what LLMs

### [01:05:47.980 - 01:05:48.980]

are

### [01:05:48.980 - 01:05:50.300]

incentivized

### [01:05:50.300 - 01:05:50.660]

in some

### [01:05:51.140 - 01:05:51.460]

sense

### [01:05:51.460 - 01:05:53.020]

so yeah

### [01:05:53.020 - 01:05:53.420]

we're thinking

### [01:05:53.420 - 01:05:53.960]

that way

### [01:05:53.960 - 01:05:55.860]

does that

### [01:05:55.860 - 01:05:56.120]

help

### [01:05:56.120 - 01:05:57.180]

cool

### [01:05:57.180 - 01:05:57.520]

cool

### [01:05:57.520 - 01:05:59.520]

any other

### [01:05:59.520 - 01:05:59.940]

question

### [01:05:59.940 - 01:06:01.060]

on this
